  - rationale: Increased UseNuke to 80 and Air to 100 in response to England's catastrophic escalation this turn. English Giant Death Robots (now 2 in Atlanta zone) and XCOM Squad paradrops on American soil create existential threat to our 3 remaining cities. Recent combat losses (Modern Armor, Mechanized Infantry, Heavy Bomber destroyed) demonstrate conventional warfare impossibility. Nuclear deterrence with 2 Uranium is becoming survival necessity, not option. Air defense at maximum priority because English Strategic Bombing continues destroying our units (Fighter bombed our Heavy Bomber). Bazooka successfully damaging XCOM Squad near Washington proves defensive capability is still viable - prioritize air/anti-air to protect cities from English air superiority. Lasers completes in 4 turns, then Stealth for Stealth Bomber - only path to asymmetric defense against England's 45:1 military advantage. Assyria (18,443 military fighting England) must be maximized as counterweight.

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1

        - DenounceWillingness: 1
        - Forgiveness: 3
        - Meanness: 1
        - Neediness: 9
        - Chattiness: 3
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Maintaining defensive persona but reducing Guarded/Afraid maximum to slightly more flexible diplomatic posture. England (73,924 strength vs America's 1,843 - 40x advantage) remains existential threat with 2 Atomic Bombs in Toledo and 2 Heavy Bombers in Atlanta. However, asymmetric combat successes (destroying 2 English Heavy Bombers, 1 Fleet Destroyer this turn) demonstrate effective defenses. NeutralBias increased from 3 to 8 signals more measured diplomatic engagement while GuardedBias reduced from 10 to 8 and AfraidBias from 10 to 8 allows for slightly more proactive cooperation with Assyria/Mongolia without appearing weak to England. Full defensive priorities maintained for Spaceship victory race.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Stealth
        - Rationale: Stealth technology confirmed as next research priority. Lasers completes in ~20 turns, Stealth immediately unlocks Stealth Bomber (game's most powerful ranged unit: 65 strength with invisibility). English military dominance (73,924 vs 1,843 - 40x) makes conventional warfare suicidal. English XCOM Squads, Giant Death Robots, and Sensor Combat Ships demonstrate technological edge. Stealth Bomber provides asymmetric defensive capability and nuclear delivery platform - essential for survival against England's strategic bombing assets. Stealth also unlocks Nuclear Fusion path for nuclear deterrence development with America's 2 Uranium. No change to research priority - trajectory validated by continuing English military escalation (2 Atomic Bombers now based in Toledo).
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Songhai relationship to neutral baseline. We captured their original capital (Gao) - they now control only Jenne. Songhai is effectively defeated (military 306 vs our 1356). Hostile modifiers are pointless against a crippled civilization. Neutral stance allows for potential peace negotiations once Gao stabilization completes, freeing military resources for Spaceship victory infrastructure and defense against The Netherlands.
        - UpdatedTurn: 278
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting The Netherlands relationship to neutral baseline (0/0). Despite active war with only 0% war weariness, Sanction: The Netherlands in place, and the Dutch being our primary diplomatic rival (15 delegates vs our 5), maintaining positive modifiers is counterproductive. They voted against Casus Belli repeal and are already pressured by multiple allies. Neutral stance reflects our opposition while leaving room for future negotiations if geopolitical conditions change.
        - UpdatedTurn: 370
        
        ### Spain
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Maintain moderate positive relationship with Assyria (+5/+5). Assyria fighting England (0% war score), controls 3 capitals, and has 15,879 military - valuable counterweight despite ideological conflict (Autocracy vs Freedom: -999 modifier) and fear of warmongering. Assyria's 26 WC votes second only to England's 12. Their vassalage of Mongolia/Egypt/Netherlands complicates diplomacy but shared war with England takes priority. Moderate positive signals cooperation without binding friendship.
        - UpdatedTurn: 435
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: 35
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum positive relationship with Mongolia (+35/+40). Mongolia (14 WC votes, 2,011 military) remains critical ally fighting England despite being Assyrian vassal (war score -100%). They absorbed English pressure on eastern front and provide Declaration of Friendship. Our Special Forces unit was gifted by Longcheng (event 20) demonstrating alliance value. England controls Karakorum (Mongolia's capital). Mongolian Military absorb English aggression while America pursues Spaceship victory. No changes needed - alliance optimally cemented.
        - UpdatedTurn: 437
        
        ### England
        - Public: -65
        - Private: -65
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum hostility toward England (-65/-65). England destroyed 3 American cities (New York, Boston, Atlanta occupied), controls 4 capitals including Karakorum (Mongolia's original capital), and continues nuclear aggression with -100% war score. England's military overwhelming at 76,819 vs America's 2,114. Recent events show England upgrading Fleet Destroyers to Sensor Combat Ships and rebasing Heavy Bomber to Beshbalik - continuing technological and military escalation. XCOM Squad paradrop capabilities and nuclear weapon possession present existential threat to remaining American cities (Washington, Philadelphia, Chicago). Absolute opposition signals survival stance while Spaceship assembly remains only victory path. No diplomatic reconciliation possible while England pursues domination victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 446
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Egypt is now an Assyrian vassal with only 929 military vs our 2,370. Previous -5/-5 hostility was for their Assyrian alignment, but as a vassal with minimal threat, negative modifiers serve no purpose. Neutral stance opens diplomatic flexibility while their vassalage to Assyria (our counterweight against England) naturally aligns their interests. Their 9 WC votes have limited utility given vassal status.
        - UpdatedTurn: 455
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Increase positive relationship with Assyria from +8/+8 to +10/+10. Assyria (18,307 military, 34 WC votes) is our key counterweight against England's overwhelming 86,479 military. Despite Autocracy vs Freedom conflict, Assyria actively fights England and provides crucial pressure to disperse English forces. Increased positive signals stronger cooperation priority while maintaining flexibility. Their diplomatic weight (34 votes) and willingness to fight England are vital for America's survival and Spaceship victory pursuit.
        - UpdatedTurn: 455
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Defensive Spaceship strategy unchanged despite heavy casualties this turn (Mobile SAM, Special Forces, Modern Armor, 2 Attack Submarines destroyed). England's Spaceship progress at 4/6 parts now with Spaceship Factories in Dover and Gao - approaching completion. Military disparity widened further: England 88,120 vs America 2,185 (40x), with 2 Giant Death Robots now in Atlanta zone, multiple XCOM Squads on American territory, and extensive Sensor Combat Ship fleet controlling seas. Bazooka successfully damaged XCOM Squad near Washington demonstrating defensive capability. Lasers to Stealth research path remains critical - though England will complete Spaceship first, Stealth Bomber provides asymmetric nuclear deterrence. Assyria (18,307 military) cannot resurrect surrendered game. Current priorities: complete Stealth for Stealth Bomber, defend remaining 3 cities, preserve military production capacity.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 7 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Samarkand:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Assyrian Sphere of Influence: Novgorod:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Longcheng:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: England:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Stockholm:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

        ### 103
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn
        - NextPlayer: 5: Egypt
        - Player: 4: America
        
        You, Washington (leader of America, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 456.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the world.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with world speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - National Treasure (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Science from Artifacts.
        - * A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
        - * Gain 250 Gold when you construct World Wonders, scaling with Era.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Current Position**
            
            I'm meticulously reviewing our current standing at turn 456 on this custom map. My focus is on the stark reality of our remaining three cities, particularly Washington, which is generating a substantial 341 production per turn.
            
            **Analyzing Dire Straits**
            
            I'm grappling with our dire situation. Despite Washington's strong production, England's overwhelming military and technological superiority, coupled with their near-complete spaceship, leave little room for optimism. I'm assessing our limited options to even prolong this.
            
            **Defending Against Escalation**
            
            I'm analyzing England's recent bomber redeployment near Washington and the approach of their Giant Death Robots and XCOM squads. This immediate military threat, combined with their imminent science victory, necessitates a critical review of our current research trajectory to see if we can pivot to defense.
            
            **Exploring Policy Options**
            
            I'm now evaluating policy choices, specifically noting the 'Stealth' tenet and its implication for Stealth Bombers. I'm reviewing available Autocracy, Tradition, and other policies to find the best fit for our current dire strategic situation.
            
            **Researching Policy Choices**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our policy path, specifically considering Autocracy given the imminent threats. `Elite Forces` could bolster our fledgling defenses, while `Total War` might boost crucial land unit production, especially as we are fielding Bazookas and Mech Infantry against England's advanced units.
            
            **Choosing Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I'm meticulously reviewing our available Autocracy tenets to bolster our defenses. Given our limited unit types and England's overwhelming might, I'm weighing `Total War` for its production boost against `Elite Forces` for combat strength. I'm also checking if we have an open level 1 slot before committing.
            
            **Selecting Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I'm now focusing on the practical application of Autocracy, specifically confirming that `Total War` is indeed a viable Level 2 tenet. With our existing Level 1 tenets secured, I can directly select `Total War` to boost our land unit production, which is critical given our current unit composition and the impending conflict. I am verifying the diplomacy with Assyria, our potential counterweight, to see if our shared past conflicts allow for improved relations.
            
            **Clarifying Ideological Conflicts**
            
            I'm untangling a confusing diplomatic report. It appears there's a significant ideology mismatch penalty (-999) with Assyria, despite their Freedom leaning and my Autocracy adoption. I'm confirming this discrepancy to understand its diplomatic impact on our strategy against England.
            
            **Revising Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm reassessing our strategic alliances given England and Assyria's unexpected peace treaty. This development significantly alters the geopolitical landscape, as England can now concentrate their full might against us and Mongolia without distraction. I need to re-evaluate our diplomatic options with Assyria to see if this new alignment can be leveraged.
            
            **Evaluating Threat Intensities**
            
            I'm reassessing the immediate military situation, noting the vast disparity between England's colossal military might and the combined forces of Mongolia and America. My focus is shifting to Washington's perilous proximity to advanced English units, including Giant Death Robots, and the overwhelming enemy strength surrounding it.
            
            **Assessing Imminent Assault**
            
            I'm analyzing the immediate threat to Washington. England's Giant Death Robots and XCOM squads are now dangerously close, with GDRs just three tiles away and XCOMs directly adjacent. I'm calculating if Washington's current defenses can withstand their imminent assault.
            
            **Prioritizing Military Production**
            
            I'm urgently reallocating our focus to maximize military unit output. Our current mobilization is far too low, so I'm pushing it to 100% to rapidly produce necessary defenses against the overwhelming enemy. I'm also ensuring our defense, city defense, and ranged unit capabilities are fully optimized.
            
            **Prioritizing Survival Now**
            
            I'm now re-prioritizing our production, shifting focus entirely to immediate military needs. With England's spaceship nearing completion and their advanced units closing in, we must maximize defense and unit production. My current goal is to leverage our limited Uranium for potential nuclear deterrents and focus all other efforts on unit output to survive the imminent onslaught.
            
            **Evaluating Spy Sabotage**
            
            I'm assessing if our spies can meaningfully delay England's impending science victory. While they are positioned in key cities, I need to confirm their sabotage capabilities against a spaceship project and consider a potential shift in our overall strategy if this is indeed our only path to survival.
            
            **Considering Victory Paths**
            
            I'm weighing the merits of a Spaceship versus a Conquest victory. While Conquest might provoke defensive AI responses, our current strategy leans heavily defensive, making Spaceship's focus on science and production more appealing. I'm now investigating diplomatic avenues with Assyria.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Parameters**
            
            I'm recalibrating our persona settings to foster alliances. My goal is to lower `GuardedBias` and `AfraidBias` while increasing `FriendlyBias` and `Friendliness` to encourage cooperation with Assyria and Mongolia. This shift should align with our strategic need for support against England.
            
            **Strengthening Foreign Ties**
            
            I'm now focusing on improving our relationship with The Netherlands. By increasing their relationship modifiers, I aim to secure a Defensive Pact that could bring them into the war against England, or at least influence their diplomatic stance, especially with their substantial delegate count.
            
            **Revising Diplomatic Modifiers**
            
            I'm re-examining our relationship modifiers with the Netherlands. Despite previously resetting them, I now realize the significant benefit of our active trade deals and existing Declaration of Friendship. I'm increasing their modifiers to ensure continued cooperation and support in World Congress.
            
            **Securing Key Vassals**
            
            I'm solidifying our standing with crucial vassals. I've reinforced our diplomatic ties with The Netherlands and Egypt, ensuring their continued trade and support by maintaining high Public and Private relationship values. This secures vital GPT and resources needed for our survival against England.
            
            **Resolving Diplomatic Friction**
            
            I'm working to overcome past mutual denunciations with Assyria. Despite ideological differences and spying incidents, I'm pushing to significantly improve our relationship modifiers. My aim is to leverage their animosity towards England to forge a critical alliance.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Postures**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our diplomatic settings, setting Assyria's relationship to Public 30, Private 30 to deter aggression and explore future options. England remains at Public -100, Private -100, solidifying our resolve against them. I'm also reviewing recent combat logs from turn 456 concerning unit movements and outcomes.
            
            **Discovering Unexpected Allies**
            
            I'm astonished to find our ally Sofia is aggressively engaging England's navy near their territory, inflicting significant damage. This unexpected support, stemming from their own defensive actions against English naval incursions, is a critical development I need to leverage. Their success is a beacon of hope.
            
            **Leveraging Naval Distractions**
            
            Sofia's bold naval assault on England's fleet near their shores presents a crucial, unexpected opportunity. I am assessing how to exploit this distraction to relieve pressure on Washington, potentially by reinforcing our own naval presence or using this window to bolster land defenses. I'm also noting our current unit positions and production, ensuring we can capitalize on this turn of events.
            
            **Adjusting Grand Strategy**
            
            I am recalibrating our overall strategic approach, prioritizing `Spaceship` victory. My focus is on enhancing defensive and mobilization flavors to 100, ensuring rapid unit production and robust city defenses. I'm also boosting ranged, anti-air, and air capabilities to counter England's aerial threats and leverage potential nuclear deterrence.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic Tools**
            
            I am now configuring the specific tools for diplomacy and policy. This involves setting relationships with key factions, adjusting our persona to foster alliances, and selecting `Total War` as our next policy to bolster production.
            
            **Refining Strategic Parameters**
            
            I'm now solidifying our strategic directives. With `Spaceship` victory as the aim, I'm ensuring our flavors prioritize defense, mobilization, and science. I've confirmed `Total War` is selected and verified that `Lasers` research remains on track.
            
            **Refining Research Path**
            
            I'm confirming `Stealth` remains our queued research, reinforcing a previously made decision. I'm also reviewing the ethical implications of nuclear weapon usage as per instructions, questioning if `UseNuke` and `Nuke` flavors should be reduced to avoid widespread devastation.
            
            **Considering Nuclear Ethics**
            
            I'm reassessing our stance on nuclear weapons, prompted by instructions regarding their ethical impact. The potential for catastrophic harm to civilians and the environment makes me consider lowering `UseNuke` and `Nuke` flavors, especially with England's atomic bombs positioned nearby.
            
            **Reducing Nuclear Reliance**
            
            I am significantly scaling back our inclination towards nuclear warfare. Given our lack of atomic bombs and the ethical implications, I'm reducing `Nuke` and `UseNuke` flavors to 10. My focus is shifting entirely to conventional asymmetric tactics, specifically Stealth Bombers and ground units.
            
            **Focusing on Defense**
            
            I am prioritizing conventional defense, setting `Nuke` and `UseNuke` flavors to 0 to avoid nuclear escalation. My focus is now on maximizing `Defense`, `CityDefense`, `Mobilization`, and `Air` flavors for Stealth Bombers, while maintaining strong `Ranged` and `Antiair` capabilities.
            
            **Adjusting Espionage Tactics**
            
            I'm now focusing on espionage to subtly influence the geopolitical landscape. With my primary focus on defense and diplomacy, I'm increasing espionage to 100, aiming to gather critical intelligence and potentially disrupt England's operations without direct confrontation. I am confirming the specific target IDs for our upcoming relationship adjustments to ensure precision.
            
            **Leveraging Espionage Gains**
            
            I'm now implementing the espionage gains to refine diplomatic stances. Specifically, I'm adjusting relationship modifiers for Assyria to foster cooperation, solidifying my alliance with Mongolia, and ensuring continued support from Egypt and The Netherlands through maintained positive relations and trade. My stance against England remains one of unyielding hostility.
